# CODEX_TASK46 — Yeni saat booster'ı + Jigo geçiş kareleri (PM_62)

Mevcut dil: T44/T45 (old money mavi + krem, doygun altın/turuncu, hacimli, siyah kontur yok). Mevcut Jigo madalyon kareleri ve saat booster kareleri `Assets/Art/_yeni/` altında (önce bul ve bak). Eski saatteki **kopuk/yamalı kol kalkıyor**.

## 1. Saat: Jigo fırçayı kılıç gibi uzatır (tek parça poz kareleri)
Madalyondaki Jigo, gövdesiyle birlikte sağa eğilip fırçasını kılıç gibi uzatır; fırça madalyondan hafifçe taşar. Kol ayrı parça gibi GÖRÜNMESİN: her kare Jigo + fırça tek parça çizim.
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/extend/01..06.png` — düğmeyi indirir, fırçayı uzatır (son kare = tam uzanmış, fırça ucu sağda).
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/hold/01..04.png` — uzanmış hâlde hafif nefes döngüsü.
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/retract/01..05.png` — fırçayı geri çeker, düğmeyi alır (son kare = normal madalyon pozuna eşit).
- Madalyon karelerinin tuvali ve pivotu mevcut normal Jigo kareleriyle aynı olsun (fırça taşan kısım için tuval sağa genişlerse `LISTE.md`'de pivot/ofseti piksel olarak yaz). Fırça ucunun kare içindeki konumunu (px) `LISTE.md`'ye yaz.

## 2. Dokunma ışıltısı + buz kaplaması
- `T46/clock/spark/01..06.png` — fırça ucunun panele değdiği yerde ışıltı (altın-beyaz yıldız patlaması).
- `T46/clock/ice_overlay.png` — Süre paneline oturan buz/ayaz kaplaması (yarı saydam, kenarları kırağılı). Panelin mevcut boyutuna göre çiz.
- `T46/clock/ice_break/01..07.png` — buz çatlar ve parçalanıp düşer.
- `T46/clock/freeze_bar_bg.png` + `freeze_bar_fill.png` — panelin altındaki 10 sn barı (buz mavisi dolgu, krem/mavi çerçeve, 9-slice kenar payını yaz).

## 3. Jigo normal ↔ mutlu geçiş + daha sık kareler
- `T46/jigo/normal_to_happy/01..05.png` ve `happy_to_normal/01..05.png` — ara kareler, ani atlama olmasın.
- Normal ve mutlu döngüleri şu an kesik görünüyor: varsa 12–24 fps için ara kareler `T46/jigo/normal_loop/`, `T46/jigo/happy_loop/` (mevcut kare sayısını 2 katına çıkar, aynı tuval).

## Teslim
`docs/art/task46/onizleme.png` (saat akışı şeridi + geçişler, 390×844 ölçekte) ve `docs/art/task46/LISTE.md` (dosyalar, kare sayıları, önerilen fps, pivot/ofset, fırça ucu konumu, 9-slice). 3 satır özet, dur. Kurallar: kurdele/afiş yok, Jigo mutlu/normalde kaş çatmaz, soluk efekt yok, RM'yi birebir kopyalama yok.

---
name: "imagegen"
description: "Generate or edit raster images when the task benefits from AI-created bitmap visuals such as photos, illustrations, textures, sprites, mockups, or transparent-background cutouts. Use when Codex should create a brand-new image, transform an existing image, or derive visual variants from references, and the output should be a bitmap asset rather than repo-native code or vector. Do not use when the task is better handled by editing existing SVG/vector/code-native assets, extending an established icon or logo system, or building the visual directly in HTML/CSS/canvas."
---

# Image Generation Skill

Generates or edits images for the current project (for example website assets, game assets, UI mockups, product mockups, wireframes, logo design, photorealistic images, or infographics).

## Top-level modes and rules

This skill has exactly two top-level modes:

- **Default built-in tool mode (preferred):** built-in `image_gen` tool for image generation, editing, and transparent-image requests. Does not require `OPENAI_API_KEY`.
- **Fallback CLI mode:** `scripts/image_gen.py` CLI. Use when the user explicitly asks for or confirms the CLI/API/model path. Requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`.

Within CLI fallback, the CLI exposes three subcommands:

- `generate`
- `edit`
- `generate-batch`

Rules:
- Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default for normal image generation and editing requests.
- Do not switch to CLI fallback for ordinary quality, size, or file-path control.
- For transparent images, ask built-in `image_gen` for a transparent background and preserve the generated alpha.
- Never silently switch from built-in `image_gen` or CLI `gpt-image-2` to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`; ask the user first unless they explicitly requested `gpt-image-1.5`.
- The word `batch` by itself does not mean CLI fallback. If the user asks for many assets or says to batch-generate assets without explicitly asking for CLI/API/model controls, stay on the built-in path and issue one built-in call per requested asset or variant.
- If the built-in tool fails or is unavailable, tell the user the CLI fallback exists and that it requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Proceed only if the user explicitly asks for that fallback.
- If the user explicitly asks for CLI mode, use the bundled `scripts/image_gen.py` workflow. Do not create one-off SDK runners.
- Never modify `scripts/image_gen.py`. If something is missing, ask the user before doing anything else.

Built-in save-path policy:
- In built-in tool mode, Codex saves generated images under `$CODEX_HOME/*` by default.
- Do not describe or rely on OS temp as the default built-in destination.
- Do not describe or rely on a destination-path argument (if any) on the built-in `image_gen` tool. If a specific location is needed, generate first and then move or copy the selected output from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...`.
- Save-path precedence in built-in mode:
  1. If the user names a destination, move or copy the selected output there.
  2. If the image is meant for the current project, move or copy the final selected image into the workspace before finishing.
  3. If the image is only for preview or brainstorming, render it inline; the underlying file can remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Do not overwrite an existing asset unless the user explicitly asked for replacement; otherwise create a sibling versioned filename such as `hero-v2.png` or `item-icon-edited.png`.

Shared prompt guidance for both modes lives in `references/prompting.md` and `references/sample-prompts.md`.

Fallback-only docs/resources for CLI mode:
- `references/cli.md`
- `references/image-api.md`
- `references/codex-network.md`
- `scripts/image_gen.py`

## When to use
- Generate a new image (concept art, product shot, cover, website hero)
- Generate a new image using one or more reference images for style, composition, or mood
- Edit an existing image (inpainting, lighting or weather transformations, background replacement, object removal, compositing, transparent background)
- Produce many assets or variants for one task

## When not to use
- Extending or matching an existing SVG/vector icon set, logo system, or illustration library inside the repo
- Creating simple shapes, diagrams, wireframes, or icons that are better produced directly in SVG, HTML/CSS, or canvas
- Making a small project-local asset edit when the source file already exists in an editable native format
- Any task where the user clearly wants deterministic code-native output instead of a generated bitmap

## Decision tree

Think about two separate questions:

1. **Intent:** is this a new image or an edit of an existing image?
2. **Execution strategy:** is this one asset or many assets/variants?

Intent:
- If the user wants to modify an existing image while preserving parts of it, treat the request as **edit**.
- If the user provides images only as references for style, composition, mood, or subject guidance, treat the request as **generate**.
- If the user provides no images, treat the request as **generate**.

Built-in edit semantics:
- Built-in edit mode is for images already visible in the conversation context, such as attached images or images generated earlier in the thread.
- If the user wants to edit a local image file with the built-in tool, first load it with built-in `view_image` tool so the image is visible in the conversation context, then proceed with the built-in edit flow.
- Do not promise arbitrary filesystem-path editing through the built-in tool.
- If a local file still needs direct file-path control, masks, or other explicit CLI-only parameters, use the explicit CLI fallback only when the user asks for it.
- For edits, preserve invariants aggressively and save non-destructively by default.

Execution strategy:
- In the built-in default path, produce many assets or variants by issuing one `image_gen` call per requested asset or variant.
- In the CLI fallback path, use the CLI `generate-batch` subcommand only when the user explicitly chose CLI mode and needs many prompts/assets.
- For many distinct assets, do not use `n` as a substitute for separate prompts. `n` is for variants of one prompt; distinct assets need distinct built-in calls or distinct CLI `generate-batch` jobs.

Assume the user wants a new image unless they clearly ask to change an existing one.

## Workflow
1. Decide the top-level mode: built-in by default, including transparent-output requests; fallback CLI only if explicitly requested or confirmed.
2. Decide the intent: `generate` or `edit`.
3. Decide whether the output is preview-only or meant to be consumed by the current project.
4. Decide the execution strategy: single asset vs repeated built-in calls vs CLI `generate-batch`.
5. Collect inputs up front: prompt(s), exact text (verbatim), constraints/avoid list, and any input images.
6. For every input image, label its role explicitly:
   - reference image
   - edit target
   - supporting insert/style/compositing input
7. If the edit target is only on the local filesystem and you are staying on the built-in path, inspect it with `view_image` first so the image is available in conversation context.
8. If the user asked for a photo, illustration, sprite, product image, banner, or other explicitly raster-style asset, use `image_gen` rather than substituting SVG/HTML/CSS placeholders. If the request is for an icon, logo, or UI graphic that should match existing repo-native SVG/vector/code assets, prefer editing those directly instead.
9. Augment the prompt based on specificity:
   - If the user's prompt is already specific and detailed, normalize it into a clear spec without adding creative requirements.
   - If the user's prompt is generic, add tasteful augmentation only when it materially improves output quality.
10. Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default.
11. For transparent-output requests, ask built-in `image_gen` for a transparent background and preserve the generated alpha channel.
12. Inspect outputs and validate: subject, style, composition, text accuracy, and invariants/avoid items.
13. Iterate with a single targeted change, then re-check.
14. For preview-only work, render the image inline; the underlying file may remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
15. For project-bound work, move or copy the selected artifact into the workspace and update any consuming code or references. Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
16. For batches or multi-asset requests, persist every requested deliverable final in the workspace unless the user explicitly asked to keep outputs preview-only. Discarded variants do not need to be kept unless requested.
17. If the user explicitly chooses or confirms the CLI fallback, then use the fallback-only docs for model, quality, size, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, output paths, and network setup.
18. Always report the final saved path(s) for any workspace-bound asset(s), plus the final prompt or prompt set and whether the built-in tool or fallback CLI mode was used.

## Transparent image requests

Ask built-in `image_gen` for a genuinely transparent background and preserve its alpha.

## Prompt augmentation

Reformat user prompts into a structured, production-oriented spec. Make the user's goal clearer and more actionable, but do not blindly add detail.

Treat this as prompt-shaping guidance, not a closed schema. Use only the lines that help, and add a short extra labeled line when it materially improves clarity.

### Specificity policy

Use the user's prompt specificity to decide how much augmentation is appropriate:

- If the prompt is already specific and detailed, preserve that specificity and only normalize/structure it.
- If the prompt is generic, you may add tasteful augmentation when it will materially improve the result.

Allowed augmentations:
- composition or framing hints
- polish level or intended-use hints
- practical layout guidance
- reasonable scene concreteness that supports the stated request

Not allowed augmentations:
- extra characters or objects that are not implied by the request
- brand names, slogans, palettes, or narrative beats that are not implied
- arbitrary side-specific placement unless the surrounding layout supports it

## Use-case taxonomy (exact slugs)

Classify each request into one of these buckets and keep the slug consistent across prompts and references.

Generate:
- photorealistic-natural — candid/editorial lifestyle scenes with real texture and natural lighting.
- product-mockup — product/packaging shots, catalog imagery, merch concepts.
- ui-mockup — app/web interface mockups and wireframes; specify the desired fidelity.
- infographic-diagram — diagrams/infographics with structured layout and text.
- scientific-educational — classroom explainers, scientific diagrams, and learning visuals with required labels and accuracy constraints.
- ads-marketing — campaign concepts and ad creatives with audience, brand position, scene, and exact tagline/copy.
- productivity-visual — slide, chart, workflow, and data-heavy business visuals.
- logo-brand — logo/mark exploration, vector-friendly.
- illustration-story — comics, children’s book art, narrative scenes.
- stylized-concept — style-driven concept art, 3D/stylized renders.
- historical-scene — period-accurate/world-knowledge scenes.

Edit:
- text-localization — translate/replace in-image text, preserve layout.
- identity-preserve — try-on, person-in-scene; lock face/body/pose.
- precise-object-edit — remove/replace a specific element (including interior swaps).
- lighting-weather — time-of-day/season/atmosphere changes only.
- background-extraction — transparent background / clean cutout. Ask built-in `image_gen` for actual transparency.
- style-transfer — apply reference style while changing subject/scene.
- compositing — multi-image insert/merge with matched lighting/perspective.
- sketch-to-render — drawing/line art to photoreal render.

## Shared prompt schema

Use the following labeled spec as shared prompt scaffolding for both top-level modes:

```text
Use case: <taxonomy slug>
Asset type: <where the asset will be used>
Primary request: <user's main prompt>
Input images: <Image 1: role; Image 2: role> (optional)
Scene/backdrop: <environment>
Subject: <main subject>
Style/medium: <photo/illustration/3D/etc>
Composition/framing: <wide/close/top-down; placement>
Lighting/mood: <lighting + mood>
Color palette: <palette notes>
Materials/textures: <surface details>
Text (verbatim): "<exact text>"
Constraints: <must keep/must avoid>
Avoid: <negative constraints>
```

Notes:
- `Asset type` and `Input images` are prompt scaffolding, not dedicated CLI flags.
- `Scene/backdrop` refers to the visual setting. It is not the same as the fallback CLI `background` parameter, which controls output transparency behavior.
- Fallback-only execution notes such as `Quality:`, `Input fidelity:`, masks, output format, and output paths belong in the CLI path only. Do not treat them as built-in `image_gen` tool arguments.

Augmentation rules:
- Keep it short.
- Add only the details needed to improve the prompt materially.
- For edits, explicitly list invariants (`change only X; keep Y unchanged`).
- If any critical detail is missing and blocks success, ask a question; otherwise proceed.

## Examples

### Generation example (hero image)
```text
Use case: product-mockup
Asset type: landing page hero
Primary request: a minimal hero image of a ceramic coffee mug
Style/medium: clean product photography
Composition/framing: wide composition with usable negative space for page copy if needed
Lighting/mood: soft studio lighting
Constraints: no logos, no text, no watermark
```

### Edit example (invariants)
```text
Use case: precise-object-edit
Asset type: product photo background replacement
Primary request: replace only the background with a warm sunset gradient
Constraints: change only the background; keep the product and its edges unchanged; no text; no watermark
```

## Prompting best practices
- Structure prompt as scene/backdrop -> subject -> details -> constraints.
- Include intended use (ad, UI mock, infographic) to set the mode and polish level.
- Use camera/composition language for photorealism.
- Only use SVG/vector stand-ins when the user explicitly asked for vector output or a non-image placeholder.
- Quote exact text and specify typography + placement.
- For tricky words, spell them letter-by-letter and require verbatim rendering.
- For multi-image inputs, reference images by index and describe how they should be used.
- For edits, repeat invariants every iteration to reduce drift.
- Iterate with single-change follow-ups.
- If the prompt is generic, add only the extra detail that will materially help.
- If the prompt is already detailed, normalize it instead of expanding it.
- For CLI fallback only, see `references/cli.md` and `references/image-api.md` for model, `quality`, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, and output-path guidance.
- For transparent images, ask built-in `image_gen` for actual transparency and preserve its alpha.

More principles shared by both modes: `references/prompting.md`.
Copy/paste specs shared by both modes: `references/sample-prompts.md`.

## Guidance by asset type
Asset-type templates (website assets, game assets, wireframes, logo) are consolidated in `references/sample-prompts.md`.

## gpt-image-2 guidance for CLI fallback

The fallback CLI defaults to `gpt-image-2`.

- Use `gpt-image-2` for new CLI/API workflows unless the user confirms a different model.
- CLI `gpt-image-2` does not support `background=transparent`; ask before using `gpt-image-1.5` unless the user explicitly requested that model.
- `gpt-image-2` always uses high fidelity for image inputs; do not set `input_fidelity` with this model.
- `gpt-image-2` supports `quality` values `low`, `medium`, `high`, and `auto`.
- Use `quality low` for fast drafts, thumbnails, and quick iterations. Use `medium`, `high`, or `auto` for final assets, dense text, diagrams, identity-sensitive edits, or high-resolution outputs.
- Square images are typically fastest to generate. Use `1024x1024` for fast square drafts.
- If the user asks for 4K-style output, use `3840x2160` for landscape or `2160x3840` for portrait.
- `gpt-image-2` size may be `auto` or `WIDTHxHEIGHT` if all constraints hold: max edge `<= 3840px`, both edges multiples of `16px`, long-to-short ratio `<= 3:1`, total pixels between `655,360` and `8,294,400`.

Popular `gpt-image-2` sizes:
- `1024x1024` square
- `1536x1024` landscape
- `1024x1536` portrait
- `2048x2048` 2K square
- `2048x1152` 2K landscape
- `3840x2160` 4K landscape
- `2160x3840` 4K portrait
- `auto`

## Fallback CLI mode only

### Temp and output conventions
These conventions apply only to the CLI fallback. They do not describe built-in `image_gen` output behavior.
- Use `tmp/imagegen/` for intermediate files (for example JSONL batches); delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/imagegen/`.
- Use `--out` or `--out-dir` to control output paths; keep filenames stable and descriptive.

### Dependencies
Prefer `uv` for dependency management in this repo.

Required Python package:
```bash
uv pip install openai
```

Optional for image inspection and downscaling:
```bash
uv pip install pillow
```

Portability note:
- If you are using the installed skill outside this repo, install dependencies into that environment with its package manager.
- In uv-managed environments, `uv pip install ...` remains the preferred path.

### Environment
- `OPENAI_API_KEY` must be set for live API calls.
- Do not ask the user for `OPENAI_API_KEY` when using the built-in `image_gen` tool.
- Never ask the user to paste the full key in chat. Ask them to set it locally and confirm when ready.

If the key is missing, give the user these steps:
1. Create an API key in the OpenAI platform UI: https://platform.openai.com/api-keys
2. Set `OPENAI_API_KEY` as an environment variable in their system.
3. Offer to guide them through setting the environment variable for their OS/shell if needed.

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it into their active environment.

### Script-mode notes
- CLI commands + examples: `references/cli.md`
- API parameter quick reference: `references/image-api.md`
- Network approvals / sandbox settings for CLI mode: `references/codex-network.md`

## Reference map
- `references/prompting.md`: shared prompting principles for both modes.
- `references/sample-prompts.md`: shared copy/paste prompt recipes for both modes.
- `references/cli.md`: fallback-only CLI usage via `scripts/image_gen.py`.
- `references/image-api.md`: fallback-only API/CLI parameter reference.
- `references/codex-network.md`: fallback-only network/sandbox troubleshooting for CLI mode.
- `scripts/image_gen.py`: fallback-only CLI implementation. Use only when the user explicitly chooses or confirms CLI mode.

2. **Execution strategy:** is this one asset or many assets/variants?

# T45 · Durdur, kayıp ve bölüm anlatımı

[Tek pafta](onizleme.png) · [Görev](../CODEX_TASK45.md)

**70 RGBA PNG**. Paftada yedi adet 390 × 844 px önce/sonra çifti ve animasyon yakın planları var. Önce ekranları yerel kod ve kaynak PNG’lerden aynı oyun arka planında yeniden kuruldu. Bomba döngüsü ayrıca PM_59 kaydından kontrol edildi. [Bomba kaydı](../task29/pm59/bomba_karti.gif)

## Önce kaynakları

| Ekran | Kullanılan yerel kaynak |
|---|---|
| Durdur | OpenPause54, ortalanan Card, T23 durdur kartı ve düğmeleri. |
| Kaybetme | Screens.Result, T23 kayıp kartı ve süre simgesi. |
| Bomba, kilit ve zincir | Screens.ObstacleCard. Bomba için mevcut 3 → 2 → 1 döngüsü, diğer ikisi için T23 sahne resimleri. |
| 1. bölüm | P21TutorialView.Story. 86 px krem kutu ve eski avatar. Önce vurgusunda Piece.shader iç renk karışımı kullanıldı. |
| Başlangıç bandı | P21TutorialView.Banner. 40 px lacivert gövde ve krem yazı. |

[GameRoot.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/GameRoot.cs) · [Screens.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Ui/Screens.cs) · [P21TutorialView.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Board/P21TutorialView.cs)

## Sonra yerleşimi

- **Durdur.** 1170 × 2532 px karartma tamamen opak. 390 × 844 örneğinde kart 356 × 464 px ve sol üstü (17, 191). Tahta alanı kartın içinde kalır. Seviye N canlı. Yeşil Devam, üç yuvarlak ayar ve küçük kırmızı Çık ayrı. Tuşların açık ve kapalı PNG’leri var.
- **Kaybetme.** Devam teklifi kapandıktan sonra Olmadı! ve canlı neden satırı kullanılır. Paftadaki örnek Süre bitti. Bomba patladı aynı yuvaya gelir. Üzgün Jigo’nun altı karesi onaylı kaynağıyla birebir. Büyük Tekrar Dene ve küçük Menü ayrı.
- **Başlıklar.** 3B Türkçe PNG’ler canlı tipografi örneği. Baloo 2 ExtraBold, krem-sarı yüz, mavi kontur, altın taban ve hafif yukarı kavis. Yıldızlı arka ışık ayrı. Gövde krem, çerçeve mavi lake.

## Engel kartları

Üç kart aynı 1044 × 1548 px paneli kullanır. Büyük engel görseli ve 840 × 268 px mini demo ayrı. Sayaç rakamları demo karelerinin sabit örnekleridir. Kart açıklaması ve Anladım! canlıdır.

| Engel | Mini demo |
|---|---|
| Bomba | 8 kare, 8 FPS. 01–02 = 3, 03–04 = 2, 05–06 = 1. 07’de parça sağa gider ve fitil söner. 08’de tahta boş, küçük duman ve tik. |
| Kilit | 8 kare, 8 FPS. 01–02 = 2, 03–04 = 1, 05 = 0. 06–08’de kilit açıkken aşağı düşer. Parça yerinde. |
| Zincir | 8 kare, 12 FPS. İki parça ve bağlantı birlikte sağa gider. Başlangıç ve bitiş kareleri kısa süre tutulabilir. |

| Kart | TR canlı açıklama | EN canlı açıklama |
|---|---|---|
| Bomba | Bombalı parçayı sayı bitmeden çıkar. Her hamlede sayı 1 azalır. 0 olursa patlar! | Clear the bomb piece before its number runs out. It drops by 1 each move. At 0 it blows! |
| Kilit | Kilitli parça N hamle boyunca hareket etmez. Başka parçaları oyna. Sayı 0 olunca kilit açılır. | A locked piece can’t move for N moves. Play other pieces. At 0 the lock opens. |
| Zincir | Zincirli iki parça birlikte hareket eder. | Two chained pieces move together. |

Kilit kartında N gerçek değerdir. Paftada 2 kullanıldı. [PM_59](../../PM_MESAJ_59.md) · [PM_60](../../PM_MESAJ_60.md) · [Texts.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Ui/Texts.cs)

## 1. bölüm ve hedef bandı

- Sohbet kutusu 1086 × 198 px. Makette 362 × 66 px, ekran yüksekliğinin %7,8’i. Alt kenarı, güvenli alan hesaba katılarak ButtonsTop’tan 10 px yukarıda. Metin canlı ve iki satıra bölünebilir. Normal ve konuşan avatar ayrı. Konuşma için onaylı mutlu kareden ağız alındı. Normal kaşlar ve gözlerin yerleşimi korunuyor.

        public bool Clock(float age, double freezeLeft, bool stopped, float safeTop)
        {
            _clock.enabled = _flyingClock.enabled = _button.enabled = _barFrame.enabled = _barFill.enabled = false;
            if (age < 0) return false;
            float s = Hud54.Scale(_screen.x), offset = Mathf.Max(0, safeTop - 37 * s);
            var center = new Vector2(227, 103) * s + new Vector2(0, offset);
            string key;
            if (age < .18f) key = "release-button";
            else if (age < .4f) key = "take-clock";
            else if (age < .65f) key = Frame("reach", 1 + Mathf.Min(2, (int)((age - .4f) * 12)));
            else if (freezeLeft > 0 && !stopped)
            {
                key = Frame("hold", 1 + (int)(Time.unscaledTime * 8) % 2);
                _clockWasActive = true; _clockEnded = -1;
            }
            else
            {
                if (!_clockWasActive) return false;
                if (_clockEnded < 0) _clockEnded = Time.unscaledTime;
                float t = Time.unscaledTime - _clockEnded;
                if (t > .6f) { _clockWasActive = false; return false; }
                key = t < .25f ? Frame("retract", 1 + Mathf.Min(2, (int)(t * 12))) : t < .45f ? "recover-button" : "idle";
            }
            Paint(_clock, "Clock/Keys/" + key, center, new Vector2(284, 210) * s);
            if (age < .4f)
            {
                float t = Mathf.Clamp01(age / .4f);
                Paint(_flyingClock, "Clock/Layers/flying-clock", Vector2.Lerp(_bar.RectOf(Booster.Clock).center, new Vector2(245, 115) * s + new Vector2(0, offset), t), Vector2.one * (48 * s), 1 - Mathf.Clamp01((t - .8f) * 5));
                Paint(_button, "Clock/Layers/button-shield", new Vector2(225, 125 + t * 70) * s + new Vector2(0, offset), Vector2.one * (36 * s), 1 - t);
            }
            if (freezeLeft > 0 && !stopped)
            {
                var p = new Vector2(324, 150) * s + new Vector2(0, offset);
                Paint(_barFrame, "Clock/Layers/bar-frame", p, new Vector2(100, 22) * s);
                float fraction = Mathf.Clamp01((float)(freezeLeft / GameSession.ClockBonusSeconds));
                Paint(_barFill, "Clock/Layers/bar-fill", p - new Vector2((1 - fraction) * 47 * s, 0), new Vector2(94 * fraction, 16) * s);
            }
            return true;
        }
    }
}

using ReverseSolver.Core;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    sealed class Hud54

        public const float PulseAmount = .045f;
        readonly TextView _left, _clock, _piecesTitle, _timeTitle;
        readonly float _scale;
        readonly JigoPortrait54 _portrait;
        public void ShowClockPortrait(bool showing) => _portrait.Visible(!showing);
        int _lastLeft = -1, _lastTime = -1;
        int _language;

        public static float Scale(float width) => width / 390f;
        static float TopOffset(float width, float safeTop) => Mathf.Max(0, safeTop - 37 * Scale(width));
        public static float TopBottom(Vector2 screen, float safeTop) =>
            Mathf.Max(screen.y * .19f + 9 * Scale(screen.x), 168 * Scale(screen.x) + TopOffset(screen.x, safeTop));
        public static float BottomTop(Vector2 screen, float safeBottom) => screen.y - screen.y * .086f - safeBottom;
        public static float ButtonsTop(Vector2 screen, float safeBottom) => BottomTop(screen, safeBottom) - 15.5f * Scale(screen.x);

        public Hud54(Transform root, Vector2 screen, float safeTop, int order, GameSession session)
        {
            _scale = Scale(screen.x);
            float offset = TopOffset(screen.x, safeTop), bottom = TopBottom(screen, safeTop);
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Top, new Vector2(screen.x / 2, bottom / 2), new Vector2(screen.x, bottom), order);
            _language = Lang.Revision;
            _left = Panel(root, 201f / 3, offset, order, out _piecesTitle, Lang.T("Pieces", "ParÃ§a"));
            _clock = Panel(root, 972f / 3, offset, order, out _timeTitle, Lang.T("Time", "SÃ¼re"));
            Art54.Place(root, Art54.Piece, new Vector2(39, 87) * _scale + new Vector2(0, offset), Vector2.one * (19 * _scale), order + 3);
            var center = new Vector2(195, 103) * _scale + new Vector2(0, offset);
            var windowSize = new Vector2(127, 104) * _scale;
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Window, center, windowSize, order + 2);
            _portrait = new JigoPortrait54(root, center, windowSize, order + 3, session);
        }

        TextView Panel(Transform root, float x, float offset, int order, out TextView title, string label)
        {
            var shift = new Vector2(0, offset);
            var body = new Vector2(113, 290f / 3) * _scale;
            var center = new Vector2(x, (181 + 145) / 3f) * _scale + shift;
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Panel, center, body, order + 1);
            Art54.Place(root, Art54.Header, new Vector2(x, (123 + 83) / 3f) * _scale + shift,
                new Vector2(364f / 3, 166f / 3) * _scale, order + 2);
            title = TextView.Create(root, "title", label, 16 * _scale, Palette.Cream, order: order + 4).Heading(TextView.RimTeal);
            title.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(new Vector2(x, 62) * _scale + shift);
            var number = TextView.Create(root, "number", "", body.y * .34f, Palette.Cream, order: order + 4)
                .Heading(TextView.RimTeal);   // same thick rim as the titles: cream fill alone vanishes on the cream body
            number.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(center);
            return number;
        }

        public void SetLeft(int n)
        {
            if (n == _lastLeft) return;
            if (_lastLeft >= 0 && n < _lastLeft && TelemetryMeta.Current?.Cohort == "p21")
                Tweens.Run(_left, .2f, t => { if (_left != null) _left.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * Mathf.Lerp(1.15f, 1, t); });
            _lastLeft = n; _left.Text = n.ToString();
        }

        public void PauseTutorialTime() { _clock.gameObject.SetActive(false); _timeTitle.gameObject.SetActive(false); }

        public void SetTime(int seconds)
        {
            int t = Mathf.Max(0, seconds);
            if (t != _lastTime)
            {
                _lastTime = t;
                _clock.Text = $"{t / 60}:{t % 60:00}";
                _clock.Color = t <= JigoMood.WorriedSeconds ? Palette.Coral : Palette.Cream;
            }
            float pulse = t <= JigoMood.WorriedSeconds ? 1 + PulseAmount * Mathf.Sin(Time.unscaledTime * Mathf.PI * 2) : 1;
            _clock.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * pulse;
            if (_language != Lang.Revision)
            {
                _language = Lang.Revision; _piecesTitle.Text = Lang.T("Pieces", "ParÃ§a"); _timeTitle.Text = Lang.T("Time", "SÃ¼re");
            }
            _portrait.Update();
        }

        public void OnEvent(GameEvent e, GameSession session) => _portrait.OnEvent(e, session);
        public Vector3 ClockPosition => _clock.transform.localPosition;
    }
}

using ReverseSolver.Core;
using UnityEngine;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    sealed class JigoPortrait54
    {
        readonly Sprite[][] _frames = new Sprite[5][];
        readonly SpriteRenderer _front, _back;
        readonly int _forced;
        readonly bool _joy;
        public void Visible(bool visible) { _front.gameObject.SetActive(visible); _back.gameObject.SetActive(visible); }
        int _current = -1, _previous, _time;
        int? _fuse;
        bool _lost, _won;
        float _happyUntil, _changedAt;

        public JigoPortrait54(Transform parent, Vector2 center, Vector2 size, int order, GameSession session)
        {
            string forced = Host.Current.Query("jigo");
            _forced = System.Array.IndexOf(Art54.Moods, forced);
            _joy = forced == "sevinc";
            for (int m = 0; m < _frames.Length; m++)
            {
                _frames[m] = new Sprite[6];
                for (int f = 0; f < 6; f++) _frames[m][f] = Art.Get((m == 4 ? "jigo-clip/" : "jigo/") + Art54.Moods[m] + "/" + (f + 1).ToString("00"));
            }
            // Frames are pre-clipped to the arch: High stripping removes SpriteMask from the player.
            var portraitSize = Vector2.one * (size.x * .85f);
            _back = Art54.Place(parent, _joy ? Art54.Joy : Art54.NormalFrame, center, portraitSize, order);
            _front = Art54.Place(parent, _joy ? Art54.Joy : Art54.NormalFrame, center, portraitSize, order + 1);
            Sync(session);
            Update();
        }

        void Sync(GameSession session)
        {
            _time = session.TimeLeft;
            _lost = session.IsOver && session.Outcome != Outcome.Win;
            _won = session.Outcome == Outcome.Win;
            _fuse = null;
            foreach (var bomb in session.Bombs)
                if (!_fuse.HasValue || bomb.Value < _fuse.Value) _fuse = bomb.Value;
        }

        public void OnEvent(GameEvent e, GameSession session)
        {
            if (e.Kind == EventKind.PieceRemoved) _happyUntil = Time.unscaledTime + JigoMood.HappySeconds;
            Sync(session);
        }

        public void Update()
        {
            if (_joy) { _back.color = Color.clear; return; }
            float now = Time.unscaledTime;
            int mood = _forced >= 0 ? _forced : (int)JigoMood.Read(_time, _fuse, now < _happyUntil, _lost, _won);
            if (mood != _current)
            {
                _previous = _current < 0 ? mood : _current;
                _current = mood; _changedAt = now;
            }
            int frame = (int)(now * JigoMood.FramesPerSecond) % 6;
            _front.sprite = _frames[_current][frame];
            _back.sprite = _frames[_previous][frame];
            float alpha = Mathf.Clamp01((now - _changedAt) / JigoMood.FadeSeconds);
            _front.color = new Color(1, 1, 1, alpha);
            _back.color = new Color(1, 1, 1, 1 - alpha);
        }
    }
}
using UnityEngine;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    // Resource keys shared by the HUD and booster bar.
    static class Art54
    {
        public const string Header = "hud/header", Top = "hud/top-strip", Bottom = "hud/bottom-strip",
            Panel = "hud/counter-panel", Piece = "hud/piece", Window = "hud/portrait-window",
            Socket = "hud/socket", Badge = "hud/badge",
            Lock = "hud/locked-icon", Pause = "hud/pause", Joy = "jigo/sevinc/01", NormalFrame = "jigo/normal/01";
        public static readonly string[] Bodies = { "hud/booster-normal", "hud/booster-pressed", "hud/booster-locked", "hud/booster-zero" };
        public static readonly string[] Settings = { "hud/settings-normal", "hud/settings-pressed", "hud/settings-locked", "hud/settings-zero" };
        public static readonly string[] Boosters = { "hud/scissors", "hud/wand", "hud/hammer", "hud/clock" };
        public static readonly string[] Moods = { "normal", "tedirgin", "korku", "mutlu", "uzgun" };

        public static SpriteRenderer Place(Transform parent, string key, Vector2 c, Vector2 size, int order)
        {
            var r = Art.Place(parent, key, size, order);
            if (r != null) r.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(c);
            return r;
        }

        public static SpriteRenderer Stretch(Transform parent, string key, Vector2 c, Vector2 size, int order)
        {
            var r = Place(parent, key, c, size, order);
            if (r != null) r.transform.localScale = new Vector3(size.x / r.sprite.bounds.size.x, size.y / r.sprite.bounds.size.y, 1);
            return r;
        }
    }
}

# CODEX_TASK46 — Yeni saat booster'ı + Jigo geçiş kareleri (PM_62)

Mevcut dil: T44/T45 (old money mavi + krem, doygun altın/turuncu, hacimli, siyah kontur yok). Mevcut Jigo madalyon kareleri ve saat booster kareleri `Assets/Art/_yeni/` altında (önce bul ve bak). Eski saatteki **kopuk/yamalı kol kalkıyor**.

## 1. Saat: Jigo fırçayı kılıç gibi uzatır (tek parça poz kareleri)
Madalyondaki Jigo, gövdesiyle birlikte sağa eğilip fırçasını kılıç gibi uzatır; fırça madalyondan hafifçe taşar. Kol ayrı parça gibi GÖRÜNMESİN: her kare Jigo + fırça tek parça çizim.
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/extend/01..06.png` — düğmeyi indirir, fırçayı uzatır (son kare = tam uzanmış, fırça ucu sağda).
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/hold/01..04.png` — uzanmış hâlde hafif nefes döngüsü.
- `Assets/Art/_yeni/T46/clock/retract/01..05.png` — fırçayı geri çeker, düğmeyi alır (son kare = normal madalyon pozuna eşit).
- Madalyon karelerinin tuvali ve pivotu mevcut normal Jigo kareleriyle aynı olsun (fırça taşan kısım için tuval sağa genişlerse `LISTE.md`'de pivot/ofseti piksel olarak yaz). Fırça ucunun kare içindeki konumunu (px) `LISTE.md`'ye yaz.

## 2. Dokunma ışıltısı + buz kaplaması
- `T46/clock/spark/01..06.png` — fırça ucunun panele değdiği yerde ışıltı (altın-beyaz yıldız patlaması).
- `T46/clock/ice_overlay.png` — Süre paneline oturan buz/ayaz kaplaması (yarı saydam, kenarları kırağılı). Panelin mevcut boyutuna göre çiz.
- `T46/clock/ice_break/01..07.png` — buz çatlar ve parçalanıp düşer.
- `T46/clock/freeze_bar_bg.png` + `freeze_bar_fill.png` — panelin altındaki 10 sn barı (buz mavisi dolgu, krem/mavi çerçeve, 9-slice kenar payını yaz).

## 3. Jigo normal ↔ mutlu geçiş + daha sık kareler
- `T46/jigo/normal_to_happy/01..05.png` ve `happy_to_normal/01..05.png` — ara kareler, ani atlama olmasın.
- Normal ve mutlu döngüleri şu an kesik görünüyor: varsa 12–24 fps için ara kareler `T46/jigo/normal_loop/`, `T46/jigo/happy_loop/` (mevcut kare sayısını 2 katına çıkar, aynı tuval).

## Teslim
`docs/art/task46/onizleme.png` (saat akışı şeridi + geçişler, 390×844 ölçekte) ve `docs/art/task46/LISTE.md` (dosyalar, kare sayıları, önerilen fps, pivot/ofset, fırça ucu konumu, 9-slice). 3 satır özet, dur. Kurallar: kurdele/afiş yok, Jigo mutlu/normalde kaş çatmaz, soluk efekt yok, RM'yi birebir kopyalama yok.

---
name: "imagegen"
description: "Generate or edit raster images when the task benefits from AI-created bitmap visuals such as photos, illustrations, textures, sprites, mockups, or transparent-background cutouts. Use when Codex should create a brand-new image, transform an existing image, or derive visual variants from references, and the output should be a bitmap asset rather than repo-native code or vector. Do not use when the task is better handled by editing existing SVG/vector/code-native assets, extending an established icon or logo system, or building the visual directly in HTML/CSS/canvas."
---

# Image Generation Skill

Generates or edits images for the current project (for example website assets, game assets, UI mockups, product mockups, wireframes, logo design, photorealistic images, or infographics).

## Top-level modes and rules

This skill has exactly two top-level modes:

- **Default built-in tool mode (preferred):** built-in `image_gen` tool for image generation, editing, and transparent-image requests. Does not require `OPENAI_API_KEY`.
- **Fallback CLI mode:** `scripts/image_gen.py` CLI. Use when the user explicitly asks for or confirms the CLI/API/model path. Requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`.

Within CLI fallback, the CLI exposes three subcommands:

- `generate`
- `edit`
- `generate-batch`

Rules:
- Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default for normal image generation and editing requests.
- Do not switch to CLI fallback for ordinary quality, size, or file-path control.
- For transparent images, ask built-in `image_gen` for a transparent background and preserve the generated alpha.
- Never silently switch from built-in `image_gen` or CLI `gpt-image-2` to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`; ask the user first unless they explicitly requested `gpt-image-1.5`.
- The word `batch` by itself does not mean CLI fallback. If the user asks for many assets or says to batch-generate assets without explicitly asking for CLI/API/model controls, stay on the built-in path and issue one built-in call per requested asset or variant.
- If the built-in tool fails or is unavailable, tell the user the CLI fallback exists and that it requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Proceed only if the user explicitly asks for that fallback.
- If the user explicitly asks for CLI mode, use the bundled `scripts/image_gen.py` workflow. Do not create one-off SDK runners.
- Never modify `scripts/image_gen.py`. If something is missing, ask the user before doing anything else.

Built-in save-path policy:
- In built-in tool mode, Codex saves generated images under `$CODEX_HOME/*` by default.
- Do not describe or rely on OS temp as the default built-in destination.
- Do not describe or rely on a destination-path argument (if any) on the built-in `image_gen` tool. If a specific location is needed, generate first and then move or copy the selected output from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...`.
- Save-path precedence in built-in mode:
  1. If the user names a destination, move or copy the selected output there.
  2. If the image is meant for the current project, move or copy the final selected image into the workspace before finishing.
  3. If the image is only for preview or brainstorming, render it inline; the underlying file can remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Do not overwrite an existing asset unless the user explicitly asked for replacement; otherwise create a sibling versioned filename such as `hero-v2.png` or `item-icon-edited.png`.

Shared prompt guidance for both modes lives in `references/prompting.md` and `references/sample-prompts.md`.

Fallback-only docs/resources for CLI mode:
- `references/cli.md`
- `references/image-api.md`
- `references/codex-network.md`
- `scripts/image_gen.py`

## When to use
- Generate a new image (concept art, product shot, cover, website hero)
- Generate a new image using one or more reference images for style, composition, or mood
- Edit an existing image (inpainting, lighting or weather transformations, background replacement, object removal, compositing, transparent background)
- Produce many assets or variants for one task

## When not to use
- Extending or matching an existing SVG/vector icon set, logo system, or illustration library inside the repo
- Creating simple shapes, diagrams, wireframes, or icons that are better produced directly in SVG, HTML/CSS, or canvas
- Making a small project-local asset edit when the source file already exists in an editable native format
- Any task where the user clearly wants deterministic code-native output instead of a generated bitmap

## Decision tree

Think about two separate questions:

1. **Intent:** is this a new image or an edit of an existing image?
2. **Execution strategy:** is this one asset or many assets/variants?

Intent:
- If the user wants to modify an existing image while preserving parts of it, treat the request as **edit**.
- If the user provides images only as references for style, composition, mood, or subject guidance, treat the request as **generate**.
- If the user provides no images, treat the request as **generate**.

Built-in edit semantics:
- Built-in edit mode is for images already visible in the conversation context, such as attached images or images generated earlier in the thread.
- If the user wants to edit a local image file with the built-in tool, first load it with built-in `view_image` tool so the image is visible in the conversation context, then proceed with the built-in edit flow.
- Do not promise arbitrary filesystem-path editing through the built-in tool.
- If a local file still needs direct file-path control, masks, or other explicit CLI-only parameters, use the explicit CLI fallback only when the user asks for it.
- For edits, preserve invariants aggressively and save non-destructively by default.

Execution strategy:
- In the built-in default path, produce many assets or variants by issuing one `image_gen` call per requested asset or variant.
- In the CLI fallback path, use the CLI `generate-batch` subcommand only when the user explicitly chose CLI mode and needs many prompts/assets.
- For many distinct assets, do not use `n` as a substitute for separate prompts. `n` is for variants of one prompt; distinct assets need distinct built-in calls or distinct CLI `generate-batch` jobs.

Assume the user wants a new image unless they clearly ask to change an existing one.

## Workflow
1. Decide the top-level mode: built-in by default, including transparent-output requests; fallback CLI only if explicitly requested or confirmed.
2. Decide the intent: `generate` or `edit`.
3. Decide whether the output is preview-only or meant to be consumed by the current project.
4. Decide the execution strategy: single asset vs repeated built-in calls vs CLI `generate-batch`.
5. Collect inputs up front: prompt(s), exact text (verbatim), constraints/avoid list, and any input images.
6. For every input image, label its role explicitly:
   - reference image
   - edit target
   - supporting insert/style/compositing input
7. If the edit target is only on the local filesystem and you are staying on the built-in path, inspect it with `view_image` first so the image is available in conversation context.
8. If the user asked for a photo, illustration, sprite, product image, banner, or other explicitly raster-style asset, use `image_gen` rather than substituting SVG/HTML/CSS placeholders. If the request is for an icon, logo, or UI graphic that should match existing repo-native SVG/vector/code assets, prefer editing those directly instead.
9. Augment the prompt based on specificity:
   - If the user's prompt is already specific and detailed, normalize it into a clear spec without adding creative requirements.
   - If the user's prompt is generic, add tasteful augmentation only when it materially improves output quality.
10. Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default.
11. For transparent-output requests, ask built-in `image_gen` for a transparent background and preserve the generated alpha channel.
12. Inspect outputs and validate: subject, style, composition, text accuracy, and invariants/avoid items.
13. Iterate with a single targeted change, then re-check.
14. For preview-only work, render the image inline; the underlying file may remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
15. For project-bound work, move or copy the selected artifact into the workspace and update any consuming code or references. Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
16. For batches or multi-asset requests, persist every requested deliverable final in the workspace unless the user explicitly asked to keep outputs preview-only. Discarded variants do not need to be kept unless requested.
17. If the user explicitly chooses or confirms the CLI fallback, then use the fallback-only docs for model, quality, size, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, output paths, and network setup.
18. Always report the final saved path(s) for any workspace-bound asset(s), plus the final prompt or prompt set and whether the built-in tool or fallback CLI mode was used.

## Transparent image requests

Ask built-in `image_gen` for a genuinely transparent background and preserve its alpha.

## Prompt augmentation

Reformat user prompts into a structured, production-oriented spec. Make the user's goal clearer and more actionable, but do not blindly add detail.

Treat this as prompt-shaping guidance, not a closed schema. Use only the lines that help, and add a short extra labeled line when it materially improves clarity.

### Specificity policy

Use the user's prompt specificity to decide how much augmentation is appropriate:

- If the prompt is already specific and detailed, preserve that specificity and only normalize/structure it.
- If the prompt is generic, you may add tasteful augmentation when it will materially improve the result.

Allowed augmentations:
- composition or framing hints
- polish level or intended-use hints
- practical layout guidance
- reasonable scene concreteness that supports the stated request

Not allowed augmentations:
- extra characters or objects that are not implied by the request
- brand names, slogans, palettes, or narrative beats that are not implied
- arbitrary side-specific placement unless the surrounding layout supports it

## Use-case taxonomy (exact slugs)

Classify each request into one of these buckets and keep the slug consistent across prompts and references.

Generate:
- photorealistic-natural — candid/editorial lifestyle scenes with real texture and natural lighting.
- product-mockup — product/packaging shots, catalog imagery, merch concepts.
- ui-mockup — app/web interface mockups and wireframes; specify the desired fidelity.
- infographic-diagram — diagrams/infographics with structured layout and text.
- scientific-educational — classroom explainers, scientific diagrams, and learning visuals with required labels and accuracy constraints.
- ads-marketing — campaign concepts and ad creatives with audience, brand position, scene, and exact tagline/copy.
- productivity-visual — slide, chart, workflow, and data-heavy business visuals.
- logo-brand — logo/mark exploration, vector-friendly.
- illustration-story — comics, children’s book art, narrative scenes.
- stylized-concept — style-driven concept art, 3D/stylized renders.
- historical-scene — period-accurate/world-knowledge scenes.

Edit:
- text-localization — translate/replace in-image text, preserve layout.
- identity-preserve — try-on, person-in-scene; lock face/body/pose.
- precise-object-edit — remove/replace a specific element (including interior swaps).
- lighting-weather — time-of-day/season/atmosphere changes only.
- background-extraction — transparent background / clean cutout. Ask built-in `image_gen` for actual transparency.
- style-transfer — apply reference style while changing subject/scene.
- compositing — multi-image insert/merge with matched lighting/perspective.
- sketch-to-render — drawing/line art to photoreal render.

## Shared prompt schema

Use the following labeled spec as shared prompt scaffolding for both top-level modes:

```text
Use case: <taxonomy slug>
Asset type: <where the asset will be used>
Primary request: <user's main prompt>
Input images: <Image 1: role; Image 2: role> (optional)
Scene/backdrop: <environment>
Subject: <main subject>
Style/medium: <photo/illustration/3D/etc>
Composition/framing: <wide/close/top-down; placement>
Lighting/mood: <lighting + mood>
Color palette: <palette notes>
Materials/textures: <surface details>
Text (verbatim): "<exact text>"
Constraints: <must keep/must avoid>
Avoid: <negative constraints>
```

Notes:
- `Asset type` and `Input images` are prompt scaffolding, not dedicated CLI flags.
- `Scene/backdrop` refers to the visual setting. It is not the same as the fallback CLI `background` parameter, which controls output transparency behavior.
- Fallback-only execution notes such as `Quality:`, `Input fidelity:`, masks, output format, and output paths belong in the CLI path only. Do not treat them as built-in `image_gen` tool arguments.

Augmentation rules:
- Keep it short.
- Add only the details needed to improve the prompt materially.
- For edits, explicitly list invariants (`change only X; keep Y unchanged`).
- If any critical detail is missing and blocks success, ask a question; otherwise proceed.

## Examples

### Generation example (hero image)
```text
Use case: product-mockup
Asset type: landing page hero
Primary request: a minimal hero image of a ceramic coffee mug
Style/medium: clean product photography
Composition/framing: wide composition with usable negative space for page copy if needed
Lighting/mood: soft studio lighting
Constraints: no logos, no text, no watermark
```

### Edit example (invariants)
```text
Use case: precise-object-edit
Asset type: product photo background replacement
Primary request: replace only the background with a warm sunset gradient
Constraints: change only the background; keep the product and its edges unchanged; no text; no watermark
```

## Prompting best practices
- Structure prompt as scene/backdrop -> subject -> details -> constraints.
- Include intended use (ad, UI mock, infographic) to set the mode and polish level.
- Use camera/composition language for photorealism.
- Only use SVG/vector stand-ins when the user explicitly asked for vector output or a non-image placeholder.
- Quote exact text and specify typography + placement.
- For tricky words, spell them letter-by-letter and require verbatim rendering.
- For multi-image inputs, reference images by index and describe how they should be used.
- For edits, repeat invariants every iteration to reduce drift.
- Iterate with single-change follow-ups.
- If the prompt is generic, add only the extra detail that will materially help.
- If the prompt is already detailed, normalize it instead of expanding it.
- For CLI fallback only, see `references/cli.md` and `references/image-api.md` for model, `quality`, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, and output-path guidance.
- For transparent images, ask built-in `image_gen` for actual transparency and preserve its alpha.

More principles shared by both modes: `references/prompting.md`.
Copy/paste specs shared by both modes: `references/sample-prompts.md`.

## Guidance by asset type
Asset-type templates (website assets, game assets, wireframes, logo) are consolidated in `references/sample-prompts.md`.

## gpt-image-2 guidance for CLI fallback

The fallback CLI defaults to `gpt-image-2`.

- Use `gpt-image-2` for new CLI/API workflows unless the user confirms a different model.
- CLI `gpt-image-2` does not support `background=transparent`; ask before using `gpt-image-1.5` unless the user explicitly requested that model.
- `gpt-image-2` always uses high fidelity for image inputs; do not set `input_fidelity` with this model.
- `gpt-image-2` supports `quality` values `low`, `medium`, `high`, and `auto`.
- Use `quality low` for fast drafts, thumbnails, and quick iterations. Use `medium`, `high`, or `auto` for final assets, dense text, diagrams, identity-sensitive edits, or high-resolution outputs.
- Square images are typically fastest to generate. Use `1024x1024` for fast square drafts.
- If the user asks for 4K-style output, use `3840x2160` for landscape or `2160x3840` for portrait.
- `gpt-image-2` size may be `auto` or `WIDTHxHEIGHT` if all constraints hold: max edge `<= 3840px`, both edges multiples of `16px`, long-to-short ratio `<= 3:1`, total pixels between `655,360` and `8,294,400`.

Popular `gpt-image-2` sizes:
- `1024x1024` square
- `1536x1024` landscape
- `1024x1536` portrait
- `2048x2048` 2K square
- `2048x1152` 2K landscape
- `3840x2160` 4K landscape
- `2160x3840` 4K portrait
- `auto`

## Fallback CLI mode only

### Temp and output conventions
These conventions apply only to the CLI fallback. They do not describe built-in `image_gen` output behavior.
- Use `tmp/imagegen/` for intermediate files (for example JSONL batches); delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/imagegen/`.
- Use `--out` or `--out-dir` to control output paths; keep filenames stable and descriptive.

### Dependencies
Prefer `uv` for dependency management in this repo.

Required Python package:
```bash
uv pip install openai
```

Optional for image inspection and downscaling:
```bash
uv pip install pillow
```

Portability note:
- If you are using the installed skill outside this repo, install dependencies into that environment with its package manager.
- In uv-managed environments, `uv pip install ...` remains the preferred path.

### Environment
- `OPENAI_API_KEY` must be set for live API calls.
- Do not ask the user for `OPENAI_API_KEY` when using the built-in `image_gen` tool.
- Never ask the user to paste the full key in chat. Ask them to set it locally and confirm when ready.

If the key is missing, give the user these steps:
1. Create an API key in the OpenAI platform UI: https://platform.openai.com/api-keys
2. Set `OPENAI_API_KEY` as an environment variable in their system.
3. Offer to guide them through setting the environment variable for their OS/shell if needed.

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it into their active environment.

### Script-mode notes
- CLI commands + examples: `references/cli.md`
- API parameter quick reference: `references/image-api.md`
- Network approvals / sandbox settings for CLI mode: `references/codex-network.md`

## Reference map
- `references/prompting.md`: shared prompting principles for both modes.
- `references/sample-prompts.md`: shared copy/paste prompt recipes for both modes.
- `references/cli.md`: fallback-only CLI usage via `scripts/image_gen.py`.
- `references/image-api.md`: fallback-only API/CLI parameter reference.
- `references/codex-network.md`: fallback-only network/sandbox troubleshooting for CLI mode.
- `scripts/image_gen.py`: fallback-only CLI implementation. Use only when the user explicitly chooses or confirms CLI mode.

# T45 · Durdur, kayıp ve bölüm anlatımı

[Tek pafta](onizleme.png) · [Görev](../CODEX_TASK45.md)

**70 RGBA PNG**. Paftada yedi adet 390 × 844 px önce/sonra çifti ve animasyon yakın planları var. Önce ekranları yerel kod ve kaynak PNG’lerden aynı oyun arka planında yeniden kuruldu. Bomba döngüsü ayrıca PM_59 kaydından kontrol edildi. [Bomba kaydı](../task29/pm59/bomba_karti.gif)

## Önce kaynakları

| Ekran | Kullanılan yerel kaynak |
|---|---|
| Durdur | OpenPause54, ortalanan Card, T23 durdur kartı ve düğmeleri. |
| Kaybetme | Screens.Result, T23 kayıp kartı ve süre simgesi. |
| Bomba, kilit ve zincir | Screens.ObstacleCard. Bomba için mevcut 3 → 2 → 1 döngüsü, diğer ikisi için T23 sahne resimleri. |
| 1. bölüm | P21TutorialView.Story. 86 px krem kutu ve eski avatar. Önce vurgusunda Piece.shader iç renk karışımı kullanıldı. |
| Başlangıç bandı | P21TutorialView.Banner. 40 px lacivert gövde ve krem yazı. |

[GameRoot.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/GameRoot.cs) · [Screens.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Ui/Screens.cs) · [P21TutorialView.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Board/P21TutorialView.cs)

## Sonra yerleşimi

- **Durdur.** 1170 × 2532 px karartma tamamen opak. 390 × 844 örneğinde kart 356 × 464 px ve sol üstü (17, 191). Tahta alanı kartın içinde kalır. Seviye N canlı. Yeşil Devam, üç yuvarlak ayar ve küçük kırmızı Çık ayrı. Tuşların açık ve kapalı PNG’leri var.
- **Kaybetme.** Devam teklifi kapandıktan sonra Olmadı! ve canlı neden satırı kullanılır. Paftadaki örnek Süre bitti. Bomba patladı aynı yuvaya gelir. Üzgün Jigo’nun altı karesi onaylı kaynağıyla birebir. Büyük Tekrar Dene ve küçük Menü ayrı.
- **Başlıklar.** 3B Türkçe PNG’ler canlı tipografi örneği. Baloo 2 ExtraBold, krem-sarı yüz, mavi kontur, altın taban ve hafif yukarı kavis. Yıldızlı arka ışık ayrı. Gövde krem, çerçeve mavi lake.

## Engel kartları

Üç kart aynı 1044 × 1548 px paneli kullanır. Büyük engel görseli ve 840 × 268 px mini demo ayrı. Sayaç rakamları demo karelerinin sabit örnekleridir. Kart açıklaması ve Anladım! canlıdır.

| Engel | Mini demo |
|---|---|
| Bomba | 8 kare, 8 FPS. 01–02 = 3, 03–04 = 2, 05–06 = 1. 07’de parça sağa gider ve fitil söner. 08’de tahta boş, küçük duman ve tik. |
| Kilit | 8 kare, 8 FPS. 01–02 = 2, 03–04 = 1, 05 = 0. 06–08’de kilit açıkken aşağı düşer. Parça yerinde. |
| Zincir | 8 kare, 12 FPS. İki parça ve bağlantı birlikte sağa gider. Başlangıç ve bitiş kareleri kısa süre tutulabilir. |

| Kart | TR canlı açıklama | EN canlı açıklama |
|---|---|---|
| Bomba | Bombalı parçayı sayı bitmeden çıkar. Her hamlede sayı 1 azalır. 0 olursa patlar! | Clear the bomb piece before its number runs out. It drops by 1 each move. At 0 it blows! |
| Kilit | Kilitli parça N hamle boyunca hareket etmez. Başka parçaları oyna. Sayı 0 olunca kilit açılır. | A locked piece can’t move for N moves. Play other pieces. At 0 the lock opens. |
| Zincir | Zincirli iki parça birlikte hareket eder. | Two chained pieces move together. |

Kilit kartında N gerçek değerdir. Paftada 2 kullanıldı. [PM_59](../../PM_MESAJ_59.md) · [PM_60](../../PM_MESAJ_60.md) · [Texts.cs](../../../Assets/_Game/Presentation/Ui/Texts.cs)

Normal ve konuşan avatar ayrı. Konuşma için onaylı mutlu kareden ağız alındı. Normal kaşlar ve gözlerin yerleşimi korunuyor.

        public bool Clock(float age, double freezeLeft, bool stopped, float safeTop)
        {
            _clock.enabled = _flyingClock.enabled = _button.enabled = _barFrame.enabled = _barFill.enabled = false;
            if (age < 0) return false;
            float s = Hud54.Scale(_screen.x), offset = Mathf.Max(0, safeTop - 37 * s);
            var center = new Vector2(227, 103) * s + new Vector2(0, offset);
            string key;
            if (age < .18f) key = "release-button";
            else if (age < .4f) key = "take-clock";
            else if (age < .65f) key = Frame("reach", 1 + Mathf.Min(2, (int)((age - .4f) * 12)));
            else if (freezeLeft > 0 && !stopped)
            {
                key = Frame("hold", 1 + (int)(Time.unscaledTime * 8) % 2);
                _clockWasActive = true; _clockEnded = -1;
            }
            else
            {
                if (!_clockWasActive) return false;
                if (_clockEnded < 0) _clockEnded = Time.unscaledTime;
                float t = Time.unscaledTime - _clockEnded;
                if (t > .6f) { _clockWasActive = false; return false; }
                key = t < .25f ? Frame("retract", 1 + Mathf.Min(2, (int)(t * 12))) : t < .45f ? "recover-button" : "idle";
            }
            Paint(_clock, "Clock/Keys/" + key, center, new Vector2(284, 210) * s);
            if (age < .4f)
            {
                float t = Mathf.Clamp01(age / .4f);
                Paint(_flyingClock, "Clock/Layers/flying-clock", Vector2.Lerp(_bar.RectOf(Booster.Clock).center, new Vector2(245, 115) * s + new Vector2(0, offset), t), Vector2.one * (48 * s), 1 - Mathf.Clamp01((t - .8f) * 5));
                Paint(_button, "Clock/Layers/button-shield", new Vector2(225, 125 + t * 70) * s + new Vector2(0, offset), Vector2.one * (36 * s), 1 - t);
            }
            if (freezeLeft > 0 && !stopped)
            {
                var p = new Vector2(324, 150) * s + new Vector2(0, offset);
                Paint(_barFrame, "Clock/Layers/bar-frame", p, new Vector2(100, 22) * s);
                float fraction = Mathf.Clamp01((float)(freezeLeft / GameSession.ClockBonusSeconds));
                Paint(_barFill, "Clock/Layers/bar-fill", p - new Vector2((1 - fraction) * 47 * s, 0), new Vector2(94 * fraction, 16) * s);
            }
            return true;
        }
    }
}

using ReverseSolver.Core;
using UnityEngine;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    sealed class Hud54
    {
        public const float PulseAmount = .045f;
        readonly TextView _left, _clock, _piecesTitle, _timeTitle;
        readonly float _scale;
        readonly JigoPortrait54 _portrait;
        public void ShowClockPortrait(bool showing) => _portrait.Visible(!showing);
        int _lastLeft = -1, _lastTime = -1;
        int _language;

        public static float Scale(float width) => width / 390f;
        static float TopOffset(float width, float safeTop) => Mathf.Max(0, safeTop - 37 * Scale(width));
        public static float TopBottom(Vector2 screen, float safeTop) =>
            Mathf.Max(screen.y * .19f + 9 * Scale(screen.x), 168 * Scale(screen.x) + TopOffset(screen.x, safeTop));
        public static float BottomTop(Vector2 screen, float safeBottom) => screen.y - screen.y * .086f - safeBottom;
        public static float ButtonsTop(Vector2 screen, float safeBottom) => BottomTop(screen, safeBottom) - 15.5f * Scale(screen.x);

        public Hud54(Transform root, Vector2 screen, float safeTop, int order, GameSession session)
        {
            _scale = Scale(screen.x);
            float offset = TopOffset(screen.x, safeTop), bottom = TopBottom(screen, safeTop);
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Top, new Vector2(screen.x / 2, bottom / 2), new Vector2(screen.x, bottom), order);
            _language = Lang.Revision;
            _left = Panel(root, 201f / 3, offset, order, out _piecesTitle, Lang.T("Pieces", "ParÃ§a"));
            _clock = Panel(root, 972f / 3, offset, order, out _timeTitle, Lang.T("Time", "SÃ¼re"));
            Art54.Place(root, Art54.Piece, new Vector2(39, 87) * _scale + new Vector2(0, offset), Vector2.one * (19 * _scale), order + 3);
            var center = new Vector2(195, 103) * _scale + new Vector2(0, offset);
            var windowSize = new Vector2(127, 104) * _scale;
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Window, center, windowSize, order + 2);
            _portrait = new JigoPortrait54(root, center, windowSize, order + 3, session);
        }

        TextView Panel(Transform root, float x, float offset, int order, out TextView title, string label)
        {
            var shift = new Vector2(0, offset);
            var body = new Vector2(113, 290f / 3) * _scale;
            var center = new Vector2(x, (181 + 145) / 3f) * _scale + shift;
            Art54.Stretch(root, Art54.Panel, center, body, order + 1);
            Art54.Place(root, Art54.Header, new Vector2(x, (123 + 83) / 3f) * _scale + shift,
                new Vector2(364f / 3, 166f / 3) * _scale, order + 2);
            title = TextView.Create(root, "title", label, 16 * _scale, Palette.Cream, order: order + 4).Heading(TextView.RimTeal);
            title.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(new Vector2(x, 62) * _scale + shift);
            var number = TextView.Create(root, "number", "", body.y * .34f, Palette.Cream, order: order + 4)
                .Heading(TextView.RimTeal);   // same thick rim as the titles: cream fill alone vanishes on the cream body
            number.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(center);
            return number;
        }

        public void SetLeft(int n)
        {
            if (n == _lastLeft) return;
            if (_lastLeft >= 0 && n < _lastLeft && TelemetryMeta.Current?.Cohort == "p21")
                Tweens.Run(_left, .2f, t => { if (_left != null) _left.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * Mathf.Lerp(1.15f, 1, t); });
            _lastLeft = n; _left.Text = n.ToString();
        }

        public void PauseTutorialTime() { _clock.gameObject.SetActive(false); _timeTitle.gameObject.SetActive(false); }

        public void SetTime(int seconds)
        {
            int t = Mathf.Max(0, seconds);
            if (t != _lastTime)
            {
                _lastTime = t;
                _clock.Text = $"{t / 60}:{t % 60:00}";
                _clock.Color = t <= JigoMood.WorriedSeconds ? Palette.Coral : Palette.Cream;
            }
            float pulse = t <= JigoMood.WorriedSeconds ? 1 + PulseAmount * Mathf.Sin(Time.unscaledTime * Mathf.PI * 2) : 1;
            _clock.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * pulse;
            if (_language != Lang.Revision)
            {
                _language = Lang.Revision; _piecesTitle.Text = Lang.T("Pieces", "ParÃ§a"); _timeTitle.Text = Lang.T("Time", "SÃ¼re");
            }
            _portrait.Update();
        }

        public void OnEvent(GameEvent e, GameSession session) => _portrait.OnEvent(e, session);
        public Vector3 ClockPosition => _clock.transform.localPosition;
    }
}

  ### Haz efektleri · 72 PNG
  
> - Üst ve alt HUD M1 pafta bileşiminde mevcut tasarımlarla kuruldu. Dört bölgenin çerçevesi, saat barı ve çekiç çatlak
ları T41’den alındı. Değnek onayında mevcut yeşil tuş gövdesi kullanıldı. [T41 teslimi](../task41/LISTE.md) · [HUD40 ba
şlıkları](../../../Assets/Art/_yeni/HUD40/Headers/)
> - Jigo büstü, eklemli saatli kol ve düğme kalkanı yerleşik imagegen ile üretildi. Çizimler katmanlara ayrıldı ve anah
tar pozlar bu katmanlardan birleştirildi. [Jigo v3](../../../Assets/Art/_yeni/Jigo_v3/) · [Saat referansı](../../../Ass
ets/Art/_yeni/HUD36/Boosters/clock-a.png)

> Use case: identity-preserving game sprite. Generate a single genuinely transparent square PNG of the EXACT turquoise 
puzzle painter Jigo in input 1, for a masked in-game portrait rig. Preserve the same original silhouette, front-facing 
puzzle tab on right side, very short soft turquoise fabric limbs, gold-brown eyes, dimensional dimpled bronze tailor's 
thimble, and painter's wooden gold/blue paintbrush held in his own right hand on the VIEWER'S LEFT. Change the pose to 
him leaning a little toward viewer-right and glancing toward that side with gently furrowed eyebrows, a small determine
d closed smile, holding the brush near his left edge. Remove the entire cream button shield and the hand holding that s
hield. His other arm is a separately rigged element, so show only a modest right-hand side of the turquoise torso, clea
n and fully drawn down to the bottom, with no hand or protruding arm on viewer right (the jigsaw tab on viewer right is
 part of his body and remains). Do not change toy proportions, color or identity. Bust crop just below chest as in refe
rence, entire thimble and brush upper tip in frame. Soft clean upper-left light, game sprite polished shaded felt, no o
utlines. No other prop, no clock, no button, no text, no painted backdrop, no white background, actual RGBA transparenc
y. Center the bust with generous side margins and slightly more canvas underneath.
  
> **Saatli kol**
  
> Use case: stylized game asset. Generate one genuinely transparent PNG of ONLY Jigo's own left arm holding his alarm c
lock, a short anatomically readable soft toy arm with a visible flexed elbow and a distinct short forearm, for rigging 
from an upper HUD portrait. Input 1 is exact style/teal fabric/hand reference. Input 2 is clock reference: preserve the
 clearly readable warm walnut clock casing, gold twin bells, cream face, chocolate hands. Horizontal sprite, upper arm 
starts on the LEFT, short thick upper arm descends at a 30-degree diagonal toward the elbow, and short forearm bends up
 to the right about 30 degrees, to a round bare turquoise hand supporting the clock from its bottom left. Wrist is visi
ble just below the thumb. Hand has four short soft rounded fingers, all genuine same turquoise felt and small, with a t
humb visible gripping the lower left of the walnut case. Clock on right half faces front, roughly upright, about as tal

tching toy body; avoid long hose, tentacle, exaggerated curve, noodle or floating disconnected hand. Cozy bright upper-
left studio lighting, rich saturated teal matching the reference, clean edges. The arm silhouette from cut shoulder end
 to wrist remains distinct and isolated. All shapes within central 85% with fully transparent margins. Exactly one arm 
and one clock, no body, no stand, no other elements, no lettering. Actual RGBA transparency.

using ReverseSolver.Core;
using UnityEngine;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    sealed class JigoPortrait54
    {
        readonly Sprite[][] _frames = new Sprite[5][];
        readonly SpriteRenderer _front, _back;
        readonly int _forced;
        readonly bool _joy;
        public void Visible(bool visible) { _front.gameObject.SetActive(visible); _back.gameObject.SetActive(visible); }
        int _current = -1, _previous, _time;
        int? _fuse;
        bool _lost, _won;
        float _happyUntil, _changedAt;

        public JigoPortrait54(Transform parent, Vector2 center, Vector2 size, int order, GameSession session)
        {
            string forced = Host.Current.Query("jigo");
            _forced = System.Array.IndexOf(Art54.Moods, forced);
            _joy = forced == "sevinc";
            for (int m = 0; m < _frames.Length; m++)
            {
                _frames[m] = new Sprite[6];
                for (int f = 0; f < 6; f++) _frames[m][f] = Art.Get((m == 4 ? "jigo-clip/" : "jigo/") + Art54.Moods[m] + "/" + (f + 1).ToString("00"));
            }
            // Frames are pre-clipped to the arch: High stripping removes SpriteMask from the player.
            var portraitSize = Vector2.one * (size.x * .85f);
            _back = Art54.Place(parent, _joy ? Art54.Joy : Art54.NormalFrame, center, portraitSize, order);
            _front = Art54.Place(parent, _joy ? Art54.Joy : Art54.NormalFrame, center, portraitSize, order + 1);
            Sync(session);
            Update();
        }

        void Sync(GameSession session)
        {
            _time = session.TimeLeft;
            _lost = session.IsOver && session.Outcome != Outcome.Win;
            _won = session.Outcome == Outcome.Win;
            _fuse = null;
            foreach (var bomb in session.Bombs)
                if (!_fuse.HasValue || bomb.Value < _fuse.Value) _fuse = bomb.Value;
        }

        public void OnEvent(GameEvent e, GameSession session)
        {
            if (e.Kind == EventKind.PieceRemoved) _happyUntil = Time.unscaledTime + JigoMood.HappySeconds;
            Sync(session);
        }

        public void Update()
        {
            if (_joy) { _back.color = Color.clear; return; }
            float now = Time.unscaledTime;
            int mood = _forced >= 0 ? _forced : (int)JigoMood.Read(_time, _fuse, now < _happyUntil, _lost, _won);
            if (mood != _current)
            {
                _previous = _current < 0 ? mood : _current;
                _current = mood; _changedAt = now;
            }
            int frame = (int)(now * JigoMood.FramesPerSecond) % 6;
            _front.sprite = _frames[_current][frame];
            _back.sprite = _frames[_previous][frame];
            float alpha = Mathf.Clamp01((now - _changedAt) / JigoMood.FadeSeconds);
            _front.color = new Color(1, 1, 1, alpha);
            _back.color = new Color(1, 1, 1, 1 - alpha);
        }
    }
}
using UnityEngine;

namespace ReverseSolver.Presentation
{
    // Resource keys shared by the HUD and booster bar.
    static class Art54
    {
        public const string Header = "hud/header", Top = "hud/top-strip", Bottom = "hud/bottom-strip",
            Panel = "hud/counter-panel", Piece = "hud/piece", Window = "hud/portrait-window",
            Socket = "hud/socket", Badge = "hud/badge",
            Lock = "hud/locked-icon", Pause = "hud/pause", Joy = "jigo/sevinc/01", NormalFrame = "jigo/normal/01";
        public static readonly string[] Bodies = { "hud/booster-normal", "hud/booster-pressed", "hud/booster-locked", "hud/booster-zero" };
        public static readonly string[] Settings = { "hud/settings-normal", "hud/settings-pressed", "hud/settings-locked", "hud/settings-zero" };
        public static readonly string[] Boosters = { "hud/scissors", "hud/wand", "hud/hammer", "hud/clock" };
        public static readonly string[] Moods = { "normal", "tedirgin", "korku", "mutlu", "uzgun" };

        public static SpriteRenderer Place(Transform parent, string key, Vector2 c, Vector2 size, int order)
        {
            var r = Art.Place(parent, key, size, order);
            if (r != null) r.transform.localPosition = Draw.W(c);
            return r;
        }

        public static SpriteRenderer Stretch(Transform parent, string key, Vector2 c, Vector2 size, int order)
        {
            var r = Place(parent, key, c, size, order);
            if (r != null) r.transform.localScale = new Vector3(size.x / r.sprite.bounds.size.x, size.y / r.sprite.bounds.size.y, 1);
            return r;
        }
    }
}
